# 5. IDENTITY + SESSION AUTHORITY

Implement/restore a canonical server-authoritative identity subsystem.

Required properties:

- cryptographically verifiable server-issued session tokens
- expiration
- revocation
- missing-session rejection
- persona/role entitlement enforcement
- server-side authorization
- protected mutation routes
- deny-by-default authorization
- no privilege escalation through browser storage
- no trust in client-supplied role/persona/permission claims

`localStorage` may hold presentation/session hints if necessary, but it must never determine authorization.

Required regression tests:

- valid session succeeds
- malformed token fails
- expired token fails
- revoked token fails
- missing token fails
- wrong persona fails
- unauthorized role fails
- client-side role manipulation fails

---

# 6. CAPABILITY REGISTRY

Restore or complete:

- `registerCapability`
- `verifyCapability`
- `getSubsystems`
- `unregisterCapability`
- `revokeCapability`
- `restoreCapability`

Required lifecycle states:

- `STATIC_CORE_CAPABILITY`
- `DYNAMIC_CAPABILITY`
- `TEMPORARY_CAPABILITY`
- `REVOKED_CAPABILITY`

Persistence:

- file-backed initially
- atomic writes
- restart-safe
- corruption-resistant
- no partial writes
- revocation survives restart

Never allow the UI to create an effective privilege merely by changing local state.

Required regression tests:

- registration
- duplicate handling
- verification
- revocation
- restoration
- unregister
- persistence
- restart recovery
- invalid capability rejection

---

# 7. COMMAND CENTER RECONCILIATION

CommandCenter must use authoritative server state.

Required flow:

`REST initial state`
â†’ `SSE live updates`
â†’ `reconciliation`
â†’ `authoritative state`

When SSE fails:

- no fabricated capability state
- no silent fallback
- show `STALE_DATA`
- show `CONNECTION_UNAVAILABLE`
- reconcile after reconnection

Remove static capability arrays that can impersonate live state.

The frontend may render state; it must not manufacture state.

---

# 8. SCENARIO / DEMO SAFETY

`lib/core/demoGuard.ts` is a hard boundary.

For:

- `SIMULATED`
- `CONCEPT`
- demo-only execution

the system must block real network/database/device side effects unless an explicit, separately authorized test mode exists.

Scenario execution must provide:

- deterministic setup
- deterministic cleanup
- rollback on failure
- rollback on cancellation
- no leaked state between repeated runs

Acceptance:

Run the same scenario repeatedly and verify that state does not accumulate unintentionally.

---

# 9. FEEDBACK / TELEMETRY INGESTION

Feedback ingestion must be resilient.

Required:

- input validation
- PII sanitization
- EventBus delivery
- backpressure handling
- temporary queue/buffer
- AI enrichment must be non-blocking to core ingestion
- enrichment failure must not destroy the original feedback
- retry policy must be bounded
- queue recovery must be deterministic

No secret, token, password, authorization header, or unnecessary PII may enter persistent telemetry.

---

# 10. COMMUNITY BUILD INTEGRITY

Restore:

`scripts/build-community.mjs`

Build must generate:

`dist/build-manifest.json`

Manifest fields:

- artifact
- relative path
- size_bytes
- sha256_hash
- build_timestamp
- build_version
- commit_sha

Build output:

`dist/ade-community-edition.zip`

The manifest hash must be independently recomputable.

The build must fail closed if the produced artifact does not match its declared digest.

---

# 11. ADE INTERNAL COMMUNICATIONS EXCHANGE â€” ADE-ICX

## 11.1 Status

ADE-ICX is now a required first-class platform subsystem.

It must exist before the full APEX AIOPS/AIBOS marketplace rollout.

## 11.2 Purpose

ADE-ICX is the internal staff communications and organizational coordination system for the ADE ecosystem.

It covers staff operating across:

- ADE Core
- AWBULI
- PROCARTA
- ADE Infrastructure
- ADE ecosystem/platform units
- future ADE products and operational subsidiaries/units

## 11.3 Automatic staff onboarding

When an eligible person is formally onboarded/employed into an ADE organizational unit:

1. ADE identity is created/linked.
2. Organizational unit is assigned.
3. Department/team is assigned.
4. Role is assigned.
5. Communication entitlements are provisioned automatically.
6. Presence becomes available through the central system.
7. The staff member becomes discoverable according to their visibility policy.
8. Audit records are created.

Deprovisioning must occur when employment/access ends.

No stale employee access may remain active indefinitely.

---

# 12. ADE-ICX ORGANIZATION MODEL

Minimum organizational roles:

- Founder
- Executive
- Department Head
- Manager
- Team Lead
- Operational Staff

The model must be data-driven rather than hard-coded to a handful of people.

Every staff identity should support:

- full name
- current role
- previous role/portfolio history
- organizational unit
- department
- branch
- city
- country
- work email
- work contact number
- employment/engagement status
- last active timestamp
- presence state

Display must remain lightweight and non-overwhelming.

Sensitive personal data must not be exposed merely because a user can browse the staff directory.

---

# 13. ADE-ICX COMMUNICATION CHANNELS

Supported channel classes:

- `DIRECT`
- `TEAM`
- `DEPARTMENT`
- `PRODUCT`
- `ESCALATION`
- `ANNOUNCEMENT`

Supported message types:

- TEXT
- FILE
- DOCUMENT
- IMAGE
- VIDEO
- VOICE
- SYSTEM_EVENT
- ESCALATION_REQUEST

The communication layer must support:

- delivery state
- timestamps
- sender
- recipient/channel
- authorization context
- attachment metadata
- audit identifiers
- retention policy
- deletion/retention rules
- moderation/security hooks where required

---

# 14. STAFF HIERARCHY + CONTACT PROTOCOL

The system must not treat the Founder or senior executives as ordinary unrestricted direct-message targets.

Default escalation chain:

`Operational Staff`
â†’ `Team Lead`
â†’ `Manager`
â†’ `Department Head`
â†’ `Executive`
â†’ `Founder`

The exact route must be data-driven.

Normal communication should follow the user's organizational scope.

Direct access to senior leadership may exist where policy explicitly grants it, but the platform must not assume universal direct access.

Emergency/security pathways may bypass ordinary routing only under explicit policy and must generate an audit record.

Required:

- reason for escalation
- originating staff member
- organizational context
- target level
- timestamp
- escalation status
- acknowledgement
- resolution/closure
- audit trail

This is a protocol system, not merely a chat UI.

---

# 15. PRESENCE

Presence is server-derived.

Supported states:

- ONLINE
- AWAY
- BUSY
- OFFLINE

The client may signal activity but may not simply declare itself `ONLINE`.

Required:

- lastSeen
- lastActive
- heartbeat/session evidence
- disconnect detection
- stale-session handling
- server-side expiry

Presence should be lightweight in directory and messaging views.

Do not make presence a user-editable privilege signal.

---

# 16. MEDIA / FILE SECURITY

ADE-ICX must support secure sharing of:

- files
- documents
- images
- video
- voice

Required controls:

- MIME validation
- extension/content consistency checks where practical
- size limits
- authorization checks
- access control
- retention
- audit events
- safe download handling
- executable upload restrictions
- path traversal prevention
- filename sanitization
- no public-by-default private staff documents

Storage should be provider-neutral.

---

# 17. PRODUCT-WIDE IDENTITY INTEGRATION

ADE-ICX must not create a second unrelated employee identity database.

Identity authority:

`ADE Core Identity`
â†’ organizational membership
â†’ product membership
â†’ communication entitlements

Examples:

A staff member belonging to AWBULI can communicate with an authorized PROCARTA staff member.

A staff member can be reassigned between units without creating a new person identity.

A staff member's previous portfolio remains historical metadata, not an active privilege.

When employment/access ends:

- active sessions revoked
- communication privileges revoked
- directory visibility adjusted
- outstanding access reviewed
- audit preserved

---

# 18. RBAC + ABAC

Use both:

### RBAC
Role determines baseline permissions.

### ABAC
Context determines whether a specific action is allowed.

Relevant attributes include:

- staff identity
- role
- department
- organizational unit
- reporting relationship
- message channel
- resource sensitivity
- employment status
- escalation state
- security policy
- time/context where required

Authorization must be evaluated server-side.

---

# 19. AUDIT + EVENTBUS

All important ICX actions must use the canonical EventBus.

Required events include:

- `staff.onboarded`
- `staff.offboarded`
- `message.sent`
- `message.delivered`
- `message.read`
- `message.rejected`
- `presence.changed`
- `escalation.created`
- `escalation.acknowledged`
- `escalation.resolved`
- `media.uploaded`
- `media.accessed`
- `capability.revoked`

Events must not bypass the canonical EventBus.

---

# 20. GPS / GSM / IMEI / SIM / IOT BOUNDARY

These remain intentionally unimplemented.

Do NOT create fake production integrations.

Explicitly unimplemented:

- GPS/GNSS hardware ingestion
- GSM/cellular ingestion
- IMEI
- IMSI
- ICCID
- SIM state
- MSISDN
- carrier APIs
- cell tower positioning
- MQTT broker integration
- OBD-II
- CAN bus
- J1939
- fleet hardware
- geofencing backed by real device telemetry

Allowed now:

- provider-neutral interfaces
- schemas/contracts
- adapters/interfaces
- capability declarations
- simulation-only fixtures
- future integration boundaries

Not allowed:

- pretending simulated coordinates are real GPS
- pretending a generated IMEI is a device IMEI
- pretending an HTTP request is carrier/GSM telemetry
- fake hardware status presented as production telemetry

Every UI element showing future telemetry must visibly distinguish `SIMULATED`, `CONCEPT`, or `UNIMPLEMENTED`.

---

# 27. DEFINITION OF DONE

The ADE-APEX platform is not considered ready for the full APEX AIOPS / AIBOS marketplace rollout merely because a build succeeds.

The platform becomes eligible for that transition only after:

- runtime authority is reconciled,
- identity/security is authoritative,
- capability lifecycle is persistent,
- CommandCenter reflects real server state,
- ADE-ICX is operationally integrated,
- staff hierarchy and escalation are enforceable,
- presence is server-derived,
- messaging/media authorization is enforced,
- tests are real and passing,
- build integrity is verified,
- telematics boundaries remain honest,
- no duplicate runtime authority remains,
- and the final repository state can reproduce every acceptance claim.

This contract is the controlling engineering specification for the next consolidated batch.
